Let me look at card.md and check the compilation of the skeleton.

The task: STLC with locally nameless + named free variables (fvar atoms), CBV semantics, has_type with T_Abs using cofinite quantification over fvar. Need to prove strong normalization.

Classic approach: logical relation / reducibility candidates for CBV STLC. Because the semantics is CBV, the logical relation needs to account for strong normalization of the *whole* term, and the key issue: for CBV, beta reduction `(λx.t) v --> open t v` only when v is a value.

Standard approach for strong normalization: Define reducibility candidates:
- For Bool: CR = SN (terms of type Bool that are strongly normalizing... but need to handle "neutral"? For CBV STLC with only Bool, functions).

Actually a clean approach: since the language is simply typed with only Bool and arrow, and CBV strong normalization is a standard result. We can define a logical relation as:

- `R_Bool t := strongly_normalizing t ∧ ...`? Hmm, actually for Bool type, need every term of type Bool that is strongly normalizing, AND maybe closedness? Let me think about what's needed for the fundamental theorem and the beta case.

Key requirements:
1. If t : T then t ∈ R_T.
2. R_T implies SN.
3. If t ∈ R_{T1→T2} and v value with v ∈ R_{T1}, then t v ∈ R_{T2}. (Needed for application case.)
4. For the abstraction case: λx.t ∈ R_{T1→T2}, i.e., for all v value, v ∈ R_{T1} → open t v ∈ R_{T2}.
5. For the T_Abs rule, we get from the IH (with the cofinite x) that open t (fvar x) ∈ R_{T2} for all x not in L. Need to show for arbitrary value v with v ∈ R_{T1}, open t v ∈ R_{T2}. This requires substitution/instantiation lemma relating open t (fvar x) and open t v — typically when v is closed (locally closed) and v ∈ R_{T1}. So R should imply locally_closed. Then we need a lemma about substitution: if open t (fvar x) ∈ R_{T2} for all x ∉ L (cofinitely), then open t v ∈ R_{T2} for locally closed v. Hmm, how to do that? Typically use a "rename/substitution" lemma: for any locally closed v, open t v has the same SN behavior as ... hmm. Actually the logical relation should be defined on terms where the R predicate is invariant under substitution? That's harder.

Alternative: define the logical relation semantically via "all closed instances"? Since the language uses fvar atoms with cofinite quantification, the standard TLC approach:

Standard "norm" proof in TLC (Software Foundations / TAPL) uses:
```
Definition norm t := exists t', t -->* t' /\ value t'.
```
But we need strong normalization, not weak. For strong normalization we need a different approach.

Approach options:

Since SN is not preserved by multistep in the needed direction directly? Actually SN is preserved backwards along steps: SN t' and t --> t' implies SN t is not true in general (t could have other reducts), but if all reducts of t are SN then t is SN. Hmm.

Standard approach with candidates: Let me recall a clean CBV SN proof. The standard Tait-style proof for CBV STLC:

Let's think hard about the app-closure lemma since it's the crux.

We want: if R_{A→B} t1 and R_A t2 then R_B (t1 t2), where R includes lc, SN and app condition.

Components:
(a) lc (t1 t2): from lc t1, lc t2. ✓ (lc_app).
(b) SN (t1 t2): The hard part.
(c) If B = C→D: ∀ v value, R C v → R D ((t1 t2) v). Also hard.

Standard trick: prove simultaneously by induction on types? Or use a stronger formulation: define, for each type T, the "candidate" and prove by induction on T the lemma: `if t1 ∈ R_{A→B} and t2 ∈ R_A then t1 t2 ∈ R_B`. The SN part requires a nested induction on SN t1 and SN t2 derivations (since t1 ∈ R gives SN t1). Let's attempt.

Lemma app_closure: for all A B t1 t2, R (A→B) t1 → R A t2 → R B (t1 t2).
Proof by induction on B (type), and within, on SN t2 / SN t1? Let's see.

Hmm, we need to induct on the SN derivation of t1 and within induct on SN t2, or vice versa. Maybe a different formulation of R for arrow that internalizes "apply to a sequence of arguments"? Or, better: use a **Kripke-style / "hereditary" definition where the arrow condition quantifies over terms and is defined via a *single* mutual recursion**:

Define R by recursion on type:
R_Bool t := lc t ∧ SN t
R_{A→B} t := lc t ∧ SN t ∧ ∀ u, R_A u → R_B (t u)

and prove the "abstraction intro" lemma:
`abs_intro`: lc (λ.T body) → (∀ v, value v → R_A v → R_B (open body v)) → R_{A→B} (λ.T body).
Proof: lc ✓, SN ✓ (value). Need ∀ u, R_A u → R_B ((λ.body) u). 

Induct on SN u (u ∈ R_A gives SN u). Components of R_B ((λ.body) u):
- lc: from lc u and lc abs ✓.
- SN: similar SN argument: reducts of (λ.body) u are (i) (λ.body) u' with u --> u' — IH (SN u' smaller, R_A u' by closure) gives R_B hence SN ✓; (ii) open body v when u = v value — need SN (open body v), from the hypothesis R_B (open body v) (since v value ∈ R_A) → SN ✓.
- If B = C→D: need ∀ v, value v → R_C v → R_D (((λ.body) u) v).
  Now ((λ.body) u) v is a term of type D. Hmm, we need R_D of it. Consider: we know by IH (SN-induction) that R_B ((λ.body) u') for reducts u'. But for the arrow condition we need the whole thing applied to v. 

Hmm, this is the same issue in another guise: the term ((λ.body) u) v where u is a non-value stuck term. Its reductions only happen inside u (since (λ.body) u is not a value, the outer app is stuck). `R_intro`: lc t → (∀ t', t --> t' → R T t') → R T t.

So: `R_intro_neutral`: if t is not a value (or specifically t is an app / fvar / bvar, i.e., "neutral"), lc t, and all reducts in R T → R T t. Where do we need R_intro? For the arrow condition in `abs_intro`: R_D (((λ.body) u) v) for u non-value. The function part ((λ.body) u) is an app, hence not a value (a value is an abs/true/false; an app is not). So R_intro_neutral at type D applies to the term ((λ.body) u) v. Its reducts: 
   - ST_App1 with ((λ.body) u) --> ((λ.body) u'): by IH on SN u (u' smaller) R_B ((λ.body) u') which gives arrow condition R_D (((λ.body) u') v) ✓.
   - ST_App2 requires left value — no. ST_AppAbs requires left = abs — no.
   So all reducts are covered ✓. And R_intro_neutral gives R_D (((λ.body) u) v).

Also in `app_closure` (if we even need it): with the all-terms arrow candidate, app_closure is *definitional*: R_{A→B} t1 gives ∀u, R_A u → R_B (t1 u) directly! So no app lemma needed! Now the fundamental theorem. Let me define things concretely.

**Substitution.** Define:
```coq
Fixpoint subst (gamma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => gamma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst gamma t1) (subst gamma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst gamma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.
```
Note: proper capture-avoiding substitution with de Bruijn would need shifting if gamma terms contain bvars; but under the invariant that gamma maps to locally closed terms, no shifting needed. Since all our gamma terms are CR terms hence lc, fine. But careful: the typing judgment and lemmas might force us to apply subst with environments containing arbitrary terms? **Freshness / cofinite picking.** In the T_Abs case, we need an atom x ∉ L ∪ FV(t1). We need to define free variables of a term: the set/list of fvars occurring. Since atoms are nats, we can compute a "max fvar + 1" and pick x = something bigger than both max FV(t1) and max L. Define:

```coq
Fixpoint fvars (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvars t1 ++ fvars t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvars t1
  | tm_true | tm_false => nil
  end.
```
Then pick x := S (max of all elements of L ++ fvars t1) via a function `max_list`. Or simpler: use a lemma "exists x, x ∉ L ++ fvars t1" via a bound: define `bound l := fold_right max 0 l`; then x = S (bound (L ++ fvars t1)) is not in the list (since all elements ≤ bound and x > bound). Need lemmas: `In x l -> x <= bound l`. For lists of nats. Straightforward.

Alternatively use `Nat.max` over a list via fold_right.

```
SN_ind : forall P : tm -> Prop,
  (forall t, (forall t', t --> t' -> SN t') -> (forall t', t --> t' -> P t') -> P t) ->
  forall t, SN t -> P t

The premise gives an induction hypothesis as a function: for the T_Abs case, Coq generates for each recursive occurrence... Now, another concern: the `R` definition is a Fixpoint returning a function tm -> Prop. Coq's `simpl` on `R (Ty_Arrow A B) t` should unfold because R's match is on the type which is a constructor ✓. But when the type is a variable T, `destruct T` works.

Let me think about whether the definition of R as a Fixpoint is accepted: 
```coq
Fixpoint R (T : ty) : tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => fun t => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => fun t => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\ (forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.
```
Structural recursion: recursive calls R T1, R T2 on strict subterms of T. But they're inside a lambda `fun t => ...`. Coq's guard condition: the fixpoint is on T; recursive calls on T1/T2 (subterms) are allowed regardless of position? The guard checker requires recursive calls applied to structurally smaller arguments, and the recursive call `R T1` is partially applied — Coq allows partial application of a fixpoint in its body? `R T1 u` is a full application to two args but R takes one explicit arg (T) and returns a function; the recursive call is `R T1` which is structurally decreasing ✓. Coq should accept it. There might be an issue with the recursion being under a lambda, but I think it's fine.

Alternatively define R via a `Definition` with a helper... The Fixpoint should work.

Fresh atom existence: need for a finite list L, ∃ x, x ∉ L. Define a bound function on lists of nats:
```coq
Fixpoint list_max (l : list atom) : nat :=
  match l with nil => 0 | x :: xs => Nat.max x (list_max xs) end.
Lemma in_list_max : forall l x, In x l -> x <= list_max l.
Lemma fresh_notin : forall l, ~ In (S (list_max l)) l.

```coq
(* ---------- auxiliary list/freshness lemmas ---------- *)

Fixpoint fvars (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvars t1 ++ fvars t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvars t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

Fixpoint list_max (l : list atom) : nat :=
  match l with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (list_max xs)
  end.

Lemma in_list_max : forall l x, In x l -> x <= list_max l.
Proof.
  intros l; induction l as [| y ys IH]; intros x Hin.
  - destruct Hin.
  - simpl. destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (list_max ys); [apply IH; exact Hin | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom : forall l, ~ In (S (list_max l)) l.
Proof.
  intros l Hin. apply in_list_max in Hin. lia.
Qed.
```

Note: `S (list_max l) <= list_max l` is false by lia ✓.

Definition update_env (gamma : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (u : tm) : atom -> tm :=

Now the logical relation:

```coq
Fixpoint R (T : ty) : tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => fun t => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => fun t =>
      locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.

Proof. intros T; induction T; intros t H; simpl in H; tauto. Qed.
```

Lemma app_not_value : forall t1 t2, ~ value (tm_app t1 t2).
Proof. intros t1 t2 H; inversion H. Qed.
```
Inversion on value (tm_app t1 t2): the constructors conclude value (tm_abs T t), value tm_true, value tm_false — all constructor-clash → no goals ✓.

```

`SN_step`:
```coq
Lemma SN_step : forall t t', t --> t' -> strongly_normalizing t -> strongly_normalizing t'.
Proof. intros t t' Hstep Hsn. destruct Hsn as [t0 Hred]. apply Hred. exact Hstep. Qed.
```
Hmm: `destruct Hsn` — SN t is SN_intro applied to t; destructing gives Hred : forall t', t --> t' -> SN t', and possibly the term index is fixed to t. Since SN_intro : forall t, (forall t', t --> t' -> SN t') -> SN t. Destructing Hsn : SN t gives hypothesis H : forall t' : tm, t --> t' -> SN t' ✓ (the index is t). Good.

`R_step`:
```coq
Lemma R_step : forall T t t', R T t -> t --> t' -> R T t'.
Proof.
  intros T; induction T as [| T1 T2 IH1 IH2]; intros t t' HR Hstep.
  - simpl in HR. destruct HR as [Hlc Hsn]. simpl. split.
    + apply step_lc with (t := t); auto.
    + apply SN_step with (t := t); auto.
  - simpl in HR. destruct HR as [Hlc [Hsn Harr]]. simpl. split; [| split].
    + apply step_lc with (t := t); auto.
    + apply SN_step with (t := t); auto.
    + intros u Hu. 
      (* need R T2 (tm_app t' u) *)
      apply IH2 with (t := tm_app t u).
      * apply Harr. exact Hu.
      * apply ST_App1; [exact Hstep | apply R_lc; exact Hu].
Qed.
```
Careful: IH2 is `forall t t', R T2 t -> t --> t' -> R T2 t'` ✓.

`R_intro_neutral`:

Lemma R_intro_neutral : forall T t,
  locally_closed t ->
  ~ value t ->
  (forall t', t --> t' -> R T t') ->
  R T t.

    + apply SN_intro; intros t' Hs. apply R_SN with (T := Ty_Arrow T1 T2). auto.
    + intros u Hu.
      apply IH2 with (t := tm_app t u).
      * apply lc_app; [exact Hlc | apply R_lc; exact Hu].
      * apply app_not_value.
      * intros r Hs. inversion Hs as [T0 t0 v Hlc0 Hval Heq1 Heq2 | t1 t1' t2 Hst Hlc2 Heq1 Heq2 | v1 t2 t2' Hval Hst Heq1 Heq2].

```

Let me now also handle the fundamental theorem's T_Abs environment lemma:

```coq
Lemma update_env_lc : forall gamma x u,
  (forall y, locally_closed (gamma y)) ->
  locally_closed u ->
  forall y, locally_closed ((update_env gamma x u) y).
Proof.
  intros gamma x u Hg Hu y. unfold update_env.
  destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E; auto.
Qed.

Lemma update_env_R : forall Gamma gamma x T1 u,
  env_CR Gamma gamma ->
  R T1 u ->
  forall y T, update Gamma x T1 y = Some T -> R T ((update_env gamma x u) y).
Proof.
  intros Gamma gamma x T1 u [_ HR] Hu y T Heq. 
  unfold update, update_env in *.
  destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
  - apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst y. injection Heq as HT. subst T. 
    (* update_env gamma x u x = u since eqb x x = true *)
    rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. exact Hu.
  - injection Heq...? Heq : (if false then Some T1 else Gamma y) = Some T → Gamma y = Some T.
    rewrite E in Heq. simpl in Heq. 
    Have gamma' y = gamma y: unfold update_env; rewrite E; reflexivity.
    apply HR with (T := T); auto.
Qed.
```
Careful: `unfold update, update_env in *` unfolds both in hypotheses and goal. Might be messy; better to handle locally.

Definition env_CR (Gamma : context) (gamma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=

**Proof: lc_at_weaken**

```coq
Lemma lc_at_weaken : forall k t, lc_at k t -> forall j, k <= j -> lc_at j t.

  - apply lc_bvar. lia.
  - apply lc_fvar.

```

Lemma lc_at_weaken : forall k t, lc_at k t -> forall j, k <= j -> lc_at j t.
Proof.

Hmm, with `try constructor; try easy` this gets confusing. Let me instead use explicit bullets and see actual names in compiler errors. I'll just write names based on the constructor premise names. Actually, in Coq, when you `induction H` on an inductive whose premises are unnamed, the hypotheses get auto names H, H0, H1, ... and induction hypotheses are named after the *premise hypothesis*: `IHH`, `IHH0`. But if the premise is named in the constructor (like `lc_at` in `lc_abs : forall k T t1, lc_at (S k) t1 -> lc_at k (tm_abs T t1)` — unnamed), the auto-generated name is H0 etc.

Coq will name things based on the constructor's argument names when available: e.g., for `lc_bvar : forall k i, i < k -> lc_at k (tm_bvar i)`, the premises `i < k` is unnamed → `H`. For `lc_app : forall k t1 t2, lc_at k t1 -> lc_at k t2 -> ...`, premises unnamed → H, H0 (since H is the name being destructed, Coq avoids reusing it?   lc_at (S k) t -> lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t).

```coq
(** STLC call-by-value strong-normalization benchmark.
    No if-then-else, non-determinism, or recursion is included. *)

From Stdlib Require Import Arith.PeanoNat.
From Stdlib Require Import Lists.List.
From Stdlib Require Import Relations.Relation_Definitions.
From Stdlib Require Import Lia.

Module STLCNormalizationNoneHardTask.

Inductive multi {X : Type} (R : relation X) : relation X :=
  | multi_refl : forall (x : X), multi R x x
  | multi_step : forall (x y z : X),
      R x y ->
      multi R y z ->
      multi R x z.

(** Language syntax, using locally nameless binders. *)

Definition atom := nat.

Inductive ty : Type :=
  | Ty_Bool : ty
  | Ty_Arrow : ty -> ty -> ty.

Inductive tm : Type :=
  | tm_bvar : nat -> tm
  | tm_fvar : atom -> tm
  | tm_app : tm -> tm -> tm
  | tm_abs : ty -> tm -> tm
  | tm_true : tm
  | tm_false : tm.

Declare Scope stlc_scope.
Delimit Scope stlc_scope with stlc.
Open Scope stlc_scope.

Declare Custom Entry stlc_ty.
Declare Custom Entry stlc_tm.

Notation "<{{ x }}>" := x (x custom stlc_ty).
Notation "x" := x
  (in custom stlc_ty at level 0, x constr at level 0) : stlc_scope.
Notation "'Bool'" := Ty_Bool
  (in custom stlc_ty at level 0) : stlc_scope.
Notation "S -> T" := (Ty_Arrow S T)
  (in custom stlc_ty at level 99, right associativity) : stlc_scope.
Notation "$( t )" := t
  (in custom stlc_ty at level 0, t constr) : stlc_scope.
Notation "( T )" := T
  (in custom stlc_ty at level 0, T custom stlc_ty) : stlc_scope.

Notation "<{ e }>" := e
  (e custom stlc_tm at level 200) : stlc_scope.
Notation "$( x )" := x
  (in custom stlc_tm at level 0, x constr, only parsing) : stlc_scope.
Notation "x" := x
  (in custom stlc_tm at level 0, x constr at level 0) : stlc_scope.
Notation "'bvar' n" := (tm_bvar n)
  (in custom stlc_tm at level 0, n constr at level 0) : stlc_scope.
Notation "'fvar' x" := (tm_fvar x)
  (in custom stlc_tm at level 0, x constr at level 0) : stlc_scope.
Notation "'true'" := tm_true
  (in custom stlc_tm at level 0) : stlc_scope.
Notation "'false'" := tm_false
  (in custom stlc_tm at level 0) : stlc_scope.
Notation "( x )" := x
  (in custom stlc_tm at level 0, x custom stlc_tm) : stlc_scope.
Notation "x y" := (tm_app x y)
  (in custom stlc_tm at level 10, left associativity) : stlc_scope.
Notation "'lambda' ':' T ',' t" := (tm_abs T t)
  (in custom stlc_tm at level 200,
   T custom stlc_ty,
   t custom stlc_tm at level 200,
   left associativity) : stlc_scope.

(** Opening and local closure. *)

Fixpoint open_rec (k : nat) (u : tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => if Nat.eqb k i then u else tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => tm_fvar x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (open_rec k u t1) (open_rec k u t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (open_rec (S k) u t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.

Definition open (t u : tm) : tm := open_rec 0 u t.

Inductive lc_at : nat -> tm -> Prop :=
  | lc_bvar : forall k i,
      i < k ->
      lc_at k (tm_bvar i)
  | lc_fvar : forall k x,
      lc_at k (tm_fvar x)
  | lc_app : forall k t1 t2,
      lc_at k t1 ->
      lc_at k t2 ->
      lc_at k (tm_app t1 t2)
  | lc_abs : forall k T t1,
      lc_at (S k) t1 ->
      lc_at k (tm_abs T t1)
  | lc_true : forall k,
      lc_at k tm_true
  | lc_false : forall k,
      lc_at k tm_false.

Definition locally_closed (t : tm) : Prop := lc_at 0 t.

(** Call-by-value operational semantics. *)

Inductive value : tm -> Prop :=
  | v_abs : forall T t,
      locally_closed (tm_abs T t) ->
      value (tm_abs T t)
  | v_true :
      value tm_true
  | v_false :
      value tm_false.

Reserved Notation "t '-->' t'" (at level 40).

Inductive step : tm -> tm -> Prop :=
  | ST_AppAbs : forall T t v,
      locally_closed (tm_abs T t) ->
      value v ->
      tm_app (tm_abs T t) v --> open t v
  | ST_App1 : forall t1 t1' t2,
      t1 --> t1' ->
      locally_closed t2 ->
      tm_app t1 t2 --> tm_app t1' t2
  | ST_App2 : forall v1 t2 t2',
      value v1 ->
      t2 --> t2' ->
      tm_app v1 t2 --> tm_app v1 t2'
where "t '-->' t'" := (step t t').

Notation multistep := (multi step).
Notation "t1 '-->*' t2" := (multistep t1 t2) (at level 40).

(** Typing rules. *)

Definition context := atom -> option ty.

Definition empty : context := fun _ => None.

Definition update (Gamma : context) (x : atom) (T : ty) : context :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then Some T else Gamma y.

Notation "x '|->' v ';' m" := (update m x v)
  (in custom stlc_tm at level 0,
   x constr at level 0,
   v custom stlc_ty,
   right associativity) : stlc_scope.
Notation "x '|->' v" := (update empty x v)
  (in custom stlc_tm at level 0,
   x constr at level 0,
   v custom stlc_ty) : stlc_scope.
Notation "'empty'" := empty
  (in custom stlc_tm) : stlc_scope.

Reserved Notation "<{ Gamma '|--' t '\in' T }>"
  (at level 0,
   Gamma custom stlc_tm at level 200,
   t custom stlc_tm,
   T custom stlc_ty).

Inductive has_type : context -> tm -> ty -> Prop :=
  | T_Var : forall Gamma x T,
      Gamma x = Some T ->
      <{ Gamma |-- fvar x \in T }>
  | T_Abs : forall (L : list atom) Gamma T1 T2 t1,
      (forall x, ~ In x L ->
        <{ x |-> T1 ; Gamma |-- $(open t1 (tm_fvar x)) \in T2 }>) ->
      <{ Gamma |-- lambda : T1, $(t1) \in T1 -> T2 }>
  | T_App : forall Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2,
      <{ Gamma |-- $(t1) \in T1 -> T2 }> ->
      <{ Gamma |-- $(t2) \in T1 }> ->
      <{ Gamma |-- $(tm_app t1 t2) \in T2 }>
  | T_True : forall Gamma,
      <{ Gamma |-- true \in Bool }>
  | T_False : forall Gamma,
      <{ Gamma |-- false \in Bool }>
where "<{ Gamma '|--' t '\in' T }>" := (has_type Gamma t T)
  : stlc_scope.

(** ============ Added material ============ *)

```coq
(** ** Auxiliary: fresh atoms *)

Fixpoint fvars (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvars t1 ++ fvars t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvars t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

Fixpoint list_max (l : list atom) : nat :=
  match l with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (list_max xs)
  end.

Lemma in_list_max : forall (l : list atom) x, In x l -> x <= list_max l.
Proof.
  intros l. induction l as [| y ys IH]; intros x Hin.
  - destruct Hin.
  - simpl in *. destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (list_max ys); [apply IH; exact Hin | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom : forall (l : list atom), ~ In (S (list_max l)) l.
Proof.
  intros l Hin. apply in_list_max in Hin. lia.
Qed.
```

Note `destruct Hin` for In x nil — `In x nil` unfolds to False; `destruct Hin` closes ✓ (In is defined as... In x nil = False definitionally? In is inductive: In x (cons ...) and no constructor for nil, so destruct works ✓).

Let me write a small test:

```coq
From Stdlib Require Import Arith.PeanoNat.
From Stdlib Require Import Lists.List.

Inductive ty : Type := TB | TA : ty -> ty -> ty.
Inductive tm : Type := bv : nat -> tm | fv : nat -> tm | app : tm -> tm -> tm | abs : ty -> tm -> tm.
Definition atom := nat.
Definition lc (t : tm) : Prop := True.
Fixpoint open_rec (k:nat) (u t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | bv i => if Nat.eqb k i then u else bv i
  | fv x => fv x
  | app a b => app (open_rec k u a) (open_rec k u b)
  | abs T b => abs T (open_rec (S k) u b)
  end.
Definition open (t u : tm) : tm := open_rec 0 u t.
Definition context := atom -> option ty.
Definition update (G : context) (x : atom) (T : ty) : context := fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then Some T else G y.

Inductive has_type : context -> tm -> ty -> Prop :=
  | T_Abs : forall (L : list atom) G T1 T2 t1,
      (forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update G x T1) (open t1 (fv x)) T2) ->
      has_type G (abs T1 t1) (TA T1 T2)
  | T_App : forall G t1 t2 T1 T2,
      has_type G t1 (TA T1 T2) -> has_type G t2 T1 -> has_type G (app t1 t2) T2
  | T_Var : forall G x T, G x = Some T -> has_type G (fv x) T.

Lemma test : forall G t T, has_type G t T -> True.
Proof.
  intros G t T H. induction H.
  - (* T_Abs *) auto.
  - auto.
  - auto.
Qed.
Print test.
```

```coq
Lemma step_app_inv : forall t1 t2 r,
  tm_app t1 t2 --> r ->
  (exists T body v, t1 = tm_abs T body /\ r = open body v /\ locally_closed (tm_abs T body) /\ value v)
  \/ (exists t1', t1 --> t1' /\ r = tm_app t1' t2 /\ locally_closed t2)

```coq
(** ** The logical relation *)

Fixpoint R (T : ty) : tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => fun t => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => fun t =>
      locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.

Lemma R_lc : forall T t, R T t -> locally_closed t.
Proof.
  intros T. induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2]; intros t H; simpl in H; tauto.
Qed.

Lemma R_SN : forall T t, R T t -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros T. induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2]; intros t H; simpl in H; tauto.
Qed.

Lemma SN_step : forall t t', t --> t' -> strongly_normalizing t -> strongly_normalizing t'.
Proof.
  intros t t' Hstep Hsn. destruct Hsn as [Hred]. apply Hred. exact Hstep.
Qed.
```

Lemma R_step : forall T t t', R T t -> t --> t' -> R T t'.
Proof.
  intros T. induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2]; intros t t' HR Hstep.
  - simpl in HR. destruct HR as [Hlc Hsn]. simpl. split.
    + apply step_lc with (t := t); assumption.

    + intros u Hu.

Qed.

Compiles. Now add R_step and R_intro_neutral.

```coq
Lemma R_step : forall T t t', R T t -> t --> t' -> R T t'.
Proof.
  intros T. induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2]; intros t t' HR Hstep.
  - simpl in HR. destruct HR as [Hlc Hsn]. simpl. split.
    + apply step_lc with (t := t); assumption.
    + eapply SN_step; eassumption.
  - simpl in HR. destruct HR as [Hlc [Hsn Harr]]. simpl. split; [| split].
    + apply step_lc with (t := t); assumption.
    + eapply SN_step; eassumption.
    + intros u Hu.
      eapply IH2.
      * apply Harr. exact Hu.
      * apply ST_App1; [exact Hstep | apply R_lc with (T := T1); exact Hu].
Qed.
```

and

```coq
Lemma R_intro_neutral : forall T t,
  locally_closed t ->
  ~ value t ->
  (forall t', t --> t' -> R T t') ->
  R T t.

  locally_closed (tm_abs A body) ->
  (forall v, value v -> R A v -> R B (open body v)) ->
  forall u, R A u -> R B (tm_app (tm_abs A body) u).

Actually I recall Coq errors with "Cannot generalize" in some cases, but typically it works: destruct reverts hypotheses depending on B, does case analysis, reintroduces. Simplest: write two separate specialized proofs inside destruct B branches, each with its own SN induction. Let me do that:

```
Lemma abs_app : forall A B body,
  locally_closed (tm_abs A body) ->
  (forall v, value v -> R A v -> R B (open body v)) ->
  forall u, R A u -> R B (tm_app (tm_abs A body) u).
Proof.
  intros A B body Hlc Hbody u Hu.
  destruct B as [| C D].
  - (* B = Ty_Bool *)
    assert (Hmain : forall u0, strongly_normalizing u0 -> R A u0 ->
                    R Ty_Bool (tm_app (tm_abs A body) u0)).
    { intros u0 Hsn. induction Hsn as [u0 Hred IHred]. intros Hu0.
      simpl. split.
      + apply lc_app; [exact Hlc | apply R_lc with (T := A); exact Hu0].
      + apply SN_intro. intros r Hs.
        destruct (step_abs_app_inv A body u0 r Hs) as [[Heq Hval] | [u' [Hst Heq]]].
        * rewrite Heq. apply R_SN with (T := Ty_Bool). apply Hbody; assumption.
        * rewrite Heq. apply R_SN with (T := Ty_Bool).
          apply IHred; [exact Hst | apply R_step with (t := u0); assumption].
    }
    apply Hmain; [apply R_SN with (T := A); exact Hu | exact Hu].
  - (* B = Ty_Arrow C D *)
    assert (Hmain : forall u0, strongly_normalizing u0 -> R A u0 ->
                    R (Ty_Arrow C D) (tm_app (tm_abs A body) u0)).
    { intros u0 Hsn. induction Hsn as [u0 Hred IHred]. intros Hu0.
      simpl. split; [| split].
      + apply lc_app; [exact Hlc | apply R_lc with (T := A); exact Hu0].
      + apply SN_intro. intros r Hs.
        destruct (step_abs_app_inv A body u0 r Hs) as [[Heq Hval] | [u' [Hst Heq]]].
        * rewrite Heq. apply R_SN with (T := Ty_Arrow C D). apply Hbody; assumption.
        * rewrite Heq. apply R_SN with (T := Ty_Arrow C D).
          apply IHred; [exact Hst | apply R_step with (t := u0); assumption].
      + intros v Hval Hv.
        apply R_intro_neutral with (T := D).
        * apply lc_app; [apply lc_app; [exact Hlc | apply R_lc with (T := A); exact Hu0] | apply R_lc with (T := C); exact Hv].
        * apply app_not_value.
        * intros r Hs.
          pose proof (step_app_inv (tm_app (tm_abs A body) u0) v r Hs) as Hcases.
          destruct Hcases as [Habs | [Happ1 | Happ2]].
          -- destruct Habs as [T0 [body0 [v0 [Heq1 [Heq2 [Hlc0 Hval0]]]]]].
             exfalso. discriminate Heq1.
          -- destruct Happ1 as [f' [Hst Heq]].
             rewrite Heq.
             destruct (step_abs_app_inv A body u0 f' Hst) as [[Heq' Hval'] | [u' [Hst' Heq']]].
             ++ rewrite Heq'. pose proof (Hbody u0 Hval' Hu0) as Harr. simpl in Harr.
                destruct Harr as [Hl [Hsn' Harr2]]. apply Harr2. exact Hv.
             ++ rewrite Heq'. pose proof (IHred u' Hst' (R_step u0 u' Hu0 Hst')) as Harr.
                simpl in Harr. destruct Harr as [Hl [Hsn' Harr2]]. apply Harr2. exact Hv.
          -- destruct Happ2 as [v2 [Hval2 [Hst Heq]]].
             exfalso. apply app_not_value in Hval2. exact Hval2.
    }
    apply Hmain; [apply R_SN with (T := A); exact Hu | exact Hu].
Qed.
```

Wait, there's an issue with `apply R_SN with (T := Ty_Bool)` when the goal is `strongly_normalizing (open body u)` — R_SN's conclusion is `SN ?t` with T implicit as an explicit argument? R_SN : forall T t, R T t -> SN t. The T is explicit, so `apply R_SN with (T := Ty_Bool)` ✓ and the term is inferred from the goal ✓.

Also in the `B = Ty_Bool` branch, `apply Hbody; assumption` needs Hbody instantiated at v := u0 ✓, and R A u0 = Hu0 ✓. `assumption` should find both (value u0 from Hval, R A u0 from Hu0) ✓.

Another concern: in the `Hmain` assertion, `induction Hsn as [u0 Hred IHred]` — the SN induction principle: SN_ind : forall P, (forall t, (forall t', t --> t' -> SN t') -> (forall t', t --> t' -> P t') -> P t) -> forall t, SN t -> P t. So `induction Hsn` where Hsn : SN u0: gives the case with Hred : forall t', u0 --> t' -> SN t' and IHred : forall t', u0 --> t' -> P t'. The `as` pattern: the constructor SN_intro has args (t, Hred) then IH inserted. Given my earlier experience, `induction Hsn as [u0 Hred IHred]` may be wrong: the SN_intro destruct pattern was `[t0 Hred]` (2 names). ```coq
(** ** Environment interpretation *)

Definition env_CR (Gamma : context) (gamma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  (forall x, locally_closed (gamma x)) /\
  (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> R T (gamma x)).

Lemma update_env_lc : forall gamma x u,
  (forall y, locally_closed (gamma y)) ->
  locally_closed u ->
  forall y, locally_closed ((update_env gamma x u) y).
Proof.
  intros gamma x u Hg Hu y. unfold update_env.
  destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E; auto.
Qed.

Lemma update_env_R : forall Gamma gamma x T1 u,
  env_CR Gamma gamma ->
  R T1 u ->
  forall y T, update Gamma x T1 y = Some T -> R T ((update_env gamma x u) y).
Proof.
  intros Gamma gamma x T1 u [Hlc HR] Hu y T Heq.
  unfold update in Heq. unfold update_env.
  destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
  - apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst y.
    injection Heq as HT. subst T.
    rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. exact Hu.
  - apply Nat.eqb_neq in E.
    rewrite E in Heq.
    (* Heq : Gamma y = Some T *)
    apply HR in Heq.
    (* goal: R T (if Nat.eqb x y then u else gamma y) = R T (gamma y) *)
    rewrite E. exact Heq.
Qed.
```
Careful in the first case: after `subst y`, `Heq : update Gamma x T1 x = Some T`; unfolding update gives `(if Nat.eqb x x then Some T1 else Gamma x) = Some T`; but Heq was already unfolded before destruct. - E : Nat.eqb x y = true → Heq : Some T1 = Some T (since the if reduces with E). `injection Heq as HT` gives T1 = T ✓.
- E : ... = false → Heq : Gamma y = Some T ✓.
After subst y := x (from Nat.eqb_eq E where E : Nat.eqb x y = true), we get E : Nat.eqb x x = true? Substituting y := x in E gives Nat.eqb x x = true ✓ (or E is re-generalized).

Goal: R T ((update_env gamma x u) x) = R T (if Nat.eqb x x then u else gamma x). `rewrite Nat.eqb_refl` should reduce to R T u ✓ (simpl might be needed: `rewrite Nat.eqb_refl` then `exact Hu` — the if with true reduces definitionally ✓).

Second case: goal R T (if Nat.eqb x y then u else gamma y); rewrite E (the eqb = false) → R T (gamma y) ✓; Heq : Gamma y = Some T; HR Heq : R T (gamma y) ✓.

But careful: `apply HR in Heq` — HR : forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> R T (gamma x); Heq : Gamma y = Some T → HR y T Heq : R T (gamma y). - T_True : forall Gamma → `Gamma`

Now the final theorem:

```coq
Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  assert (Henv : env_CR empty (fun x => tm_fvar x)).
  { split.
    - intros x. apply lc_fvar.
    - intros x T0 Heq. unfold empty in Heq. discriminate. }
  pose proof (fundamental empty t T Hty (fun x => tm_fvar x) Henv) as HR.
  rewrite subst_id in HR.

Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  assert (Henv : env_CR empty (fun x => tm_fvar x)).
  { split.
    - intros x. apply lc_fvar.
    - intros x T0 Heq. unfold empty in Heq. discriminate. }
  pose proof (fundamental empty t T Hty (fun x => tm_fvar x) Henv) as HR.
  rewrite subst_id in HR.
  apply R_SN with (T := T). exact HR.
Qed.
```
